This mechanism is expected to be operational at the beginning of 2022 at the beginning of the year. However, in order not to wait so long – for we now have March and can't wait just nine months – we have already supported the establishment of an international independent platform of NGOs – Belarusian and European NGOs – politically and financially, collect and keep the evidence so that we can have it at our disposal if we need it, if we want to demonstrate, so to speak, the accountability and responsibility of the perpetrators in Belarus. These are not duplicates or contradictory mechanisms, but they cross into each other. I see that this international civil society platform then presents its report to the UN High Commissioner, who, if you want, continues the work “official” – under quotation marks.

We will present a study by June 2021 in which the possibility of a constitutional anchoring of the fundamental right to climate protection – a very central requirement of the climate people’s desire – will be reviewed. We will anchor a greenhouse gas budget compatible with the objectives of Paris by law. This is the central requirement of the climate people's desire, and we have agreed that we will anchor this by law – this is a completely new quality in climate protection in Austria. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Jewel beater.]] We have agreed that we will form a climate cabinet, including a legal foundation, which will not be responsible for climate protection with the Minister alone, but with the Federal Government as a whole up to the Federal Chancellor and also with the federal states. We have agreed on the establishment of a Scientific Climate Advisory Council, which will also monitor compliance with this CO2 budget. In the formulation we have held very strongly to the climate accounting court required by the climate people's desire. This scientific climate advisory board will also receive its own office. In addition, and I am pleased, we will examine whether we can extend the work of the Parliamentary Budget Service, which we already have, and the very good work for all of us here, to the task of monitoring the greenhouse gas budget. We have also set up, and I believe, an incredibly important innovation, a climate responsibility fund, or we have agreed that we want to set it up. In principle, the question is that the federal government and the countries take responsibility together and that, if we leave the climate path, instead of spending millions on penalty payments and certificates, we are implementing domestic climate protection measures. We have – Julia Herr has already mentioned it – also written in that we will continue to carry on these climate protection billions, which we have already decided this year, so that this is not a one-time effect from a Corona economic policy, but that it will be ensured in the real long term. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The request – it was distributed to you – is very detailed. I have now chosen only a few points which are not in the government programme, which go beyond the government programme or which are a major clarification. Ladies and gentlemen, we all have often asked ourselves the question – and people outside have often asked this question – whether a popular desire pays off at all, whether this commitment pays off. In my view, today's decision provides a very clear answer to this question: Every single signature, every voice, every e-mail sent, every personal conversation that was conducted, has brought us forward, has brought us to this decision. That is why I would really like to thank all those who have worked for it – starting with Katharina Rogenhofer, the initiator, all the volunteers and everyone who has signed this popular desire. – Thank you very much! Without you, there would be no such a dissuasive decision today! Thank you. We are not here, but it is about setting reasonable goals, attainable goals, not: Exit from 2025 from the combustion power engines! – That’s a goal we don’t think is reasonable. We are of course for circular economy, circular economy in the regional area. We are of course for sustainable regional agriculture et cetera. Of course, we don't want a nuclear current. Austria, by the way, is one of the few countries – and of course we are – which has no coal power plants and does not produce nuclear power. But that doesn't mean we're going to shoot and say, We do not need any gas power plants, they also belong away, because we need gas power plants for security of supply, and we also need the gas infrastructure for security of supply. When you think about it in the end: It does nothing for us if we are the world champions in climate protection and jobs are lost. In my opinion, we also need to think things over. Example ATB Spielberg, Obersteiermark: 400 jobs have been lost. Why did they get lost? – Well, because the Chinese owners have decided something. This is the first topic: Why do the Chinese owners have? The Chinese owners have decided to dismantle the infrastructure there and build it elsewhere. Where? – In Poland and Slovakia, where Poland, for example – and you know this – did not register 36 percent reduction in the Effort Sharing, but 7 percent, i.e. a competitive advantage for Poland; in which Poland, which receives billions of euros from the EU, in which Poland, which may not now increase the NOVA, wants to abolish the penalty rate and increase the fuel prices. That means, at the end of thought, this policy destroys jobs and does not create any. These are the concerns we have. It is about a balanced energy policy, and please, just in times like this! There will be huge tasks in the wake of the Corona crisis. In our view, we should really try to secure jobs and avoid anything that endangers jobs. Overwhelming goals put jobs in jeopardy. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-26-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00093_d8e9670	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Austria rejects nuclear energy. There's no If, there's no But. This goes beyond all political boundaries, and it will remain the same. Nuclear power is not an energy source of the future. The future belongs to renewable energies, which do not include nuclear energy. Nuclear energy does not provide climate protection, in which we all agree. But it is always good that people stick to it, engage in it, work for it, and keep subservient to this consensus. That is why at this point also a thank you to the many people who have signed this popular desire in the good faith – I am convinced – to the many people who are committed to nuclear power and who have also collected weekly signatures in the period before Corona, start petitions in the neighbouring states, and build civil society so that we can act even better, even stronger, against nuclear power. I would therefore also like to take the time to explain, from my point of view, why it is not possible and not useful to meet the demand of the people, following the speeches of Mr Prinz and Mr Shetty. It is not possible to leave Euratom without leaving the European Union. This has all the experts who have the Constitutional Service of the International Law Bureau. Dozens of times tested and determined again and again. I would also ask the Liberal Party to declare itself on this issue, but I believe that for all the other political groups in this House it is perfectly clear that this is not a way. The Euratom Treaty and the Treaties underlying the EU are both legal and structural and institutionally simple and simply too much intertwined than allowing a separation. If someone doesn't believe the Constitutional experts, I'd take a look at the Brexit. There we have had the opposite case: UK would like to remain in Euratom and leave the EU. It wasn't possible. The two issues are too closely interconnected as if an isolated handling of Euratom and EU would have been possible. Brexit is the practical, striking final proof of this. There is also no own Euratom budget, Mr Schmiedlechner. I would like to say quite clearly: There is no own Euratom budget! There is only the general budget of the EU. The legal arguments, why it is not possible, have already been mentioned. But why it does not make sense for nuclear policy and strategic purposes is also to be mentioned at this point. We can only say that – and several Members have already said that – if we sit at the table. We can only push for reforms if we sit at the table, and we can also prevent errors only if we sit at the table. Let me briefly mention three examples from the last weeks and months: We have prevented the reconstruction of nuclear power plants from being considered to be climate protection in accordance with the EU budget rules. We have prevented the research reactor from going through as a climate protection, and we have also prevented global EU external assistance from supporting nuclear power operators. These are three very concrete things that we have fought through, fought through and prevented in the last few weeks and months. These are three of many situations where we see that it makes a difference when Austria is sitting at the table, that it makes a difference when a country is sitting at the table, which is clearly – I say with self-confidence and conviction – acting as a pioneer in anti-nuclear policy in Europe and insisting that we reform the Treaty. That is what we are working on, and I am committed to doing so with all my strength at European level. That makes sense, that is what we should work on. That is why I thank you for your support, including today's request. We will continue to strengthen our efforts to take the next steps. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-26-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00093_d8e11418	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! First of all, I would also like to say that I, as Minister of the Interior, recognise the initiators of popular desire. People's desire is an essential element of our federal constitution within the framework of direct democracy, and every popular desire that has so much success that it is actually discussed here in the High House is a good sign of democracy and also an important sign of discussion culture. Asylum and migration policy is indeed a demanding issue – not only for the Republic of Austria, but for the whole of the European Union. From my point of view, there are three cornerstones of our policy, which are essential and deserve to be highlighted. On the one hand, there is an external dimension: The external dimension covers all that has to do with the issue of safe third countries, and there needs to be a strong, united European Union, a Commission that is willing to open negotiations. In the last Council of Ministers of the Interior, together with the Foreign Ministers, this request was also clearly addressed by a large majority to the Commission, as this objective unites all Member States of the European Union: When it comes to being able to bring back people who do not have residence rights within the European Union, efforts are needed. It sounds so technical now, but it means nothing but the following: If we are able to make it credible through return, that there is indeed an asylum and migration policy within the European Union, and it is also a consequence if we do not have an asylum ground or a right to immigration into the European Union, so that we have to leave the European Union again, we are able to make real progress in the discussion within the Member States. Therefore: Agreements with safe third countries, bundled return actions! Now Austria is constantly participating in joint return charters. At first glance, when you look at it, this may always be the hard Since an asylum policy or also a migration policy, but in fact, efficient returns also mean that the business model of organised crime, the towman who serves the suffering of people, is collapsing. We point this out in many ways, including campaigns in social media, in order to make it clear to people that they should not get on the road until they know that there is no right to stay in the European Union, that they don't pay 5 000 dollars or 5 000 euros for an uncertain crossing towards Europe and then find nothing but suffering, misery and the bitter realization that they can't stay and have entrusted themselves to the wrong ones, namely to those who tow and organized crime. Many initiatives are needed here – this happens. The Commission is much stronger than ever before willing to address these issues precisely. There is an intensive exchange, there is one with Commissioner Várhelyi who takes it seriously on this issue, she also moves forward in the Commission, as well as the Vice-Presidents of the EU-Commission for China. Also with Commissioner Johansson, there are intensive discussions and the willingness to advance and strengthen this important issue in order to make it clear that we are all working together in the European Union to ensure that organised crime and towmaking will lose the business base in the future. The second cornerstone is effective border protection from our point of view. However, it does not start at the Austrian border, but according to our three-security network strategy, it is the first safety network against illegal, irregular migration, against the organised crime of border protection at the EU's external border. There are also strong efforts to expand Frontex – this is the European Border Protection Agency – and to support nationally in addition, just as Austria did, when Greece was put into distress by Turkey. These were dramatic scenes at the border between Greece and Turkey almost a year ago, as violent irregular migrants who were deliberately led by Turkey there, with the support of Turkish security authorities trying to break the border fence. Tear gas grenades have been fired on European territory, and Austria has shown by its solidarity in police terms that Greece is not left alone. However, the Austrian strategy of safety networks continues. It is not just the EU's external border protection, it is the border protection of the Western Balkans, which has already been mentioned today, which have been particularly challenged. Thanks to the commitment of the states of the Western Balkans, it is due to the fact that we are not currently feeling a massive wave of migration, because even there the authorities are trying to leave the irregular migrants there in fact. We also need to draw a signal here, too, that Bosnia and Herzegovina, Serbia, Northern Macedonia will not be left alone, and that an EU border country such as Croatia also feels our solidarity, because the Croatians are currently carrying out an intensive and closed fight against organised crime, against towing, and this must be supported. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The third important area of the safety net is then its own border protection. I'm just coming from the Burgenland-Hungarian border. Together with the Minister of Defense, we have accompanied a commitment in which, in the assistance of the Austrian Federal Army, in a partnership that has been used for more than 30 years, it is about fighting tow, organized crime. It has succeeded in significantly increasing the arrest rate of tractors carrying out a careless business – whether in the course of trafficking in human beings or in the case of irregular migration. We have been able to increase the rate by 30 percent. This is the merit of the soldiers, the police, who make an essential contribution to the fight against organised crime, which is also firmly continued. This fight always has an international dimension, because organized crime is boundless, is multinational and must also be fought multinationally. The third cornerstone of the Security Strategy, on asylum and migration, is the question of how we ourselves deal with the proposals that have already been addressed today, with the EU Commission's proposals on the asylum and migration pact. We have made it quite clear to the European Commission that we agree with many ideas and proposals to the greatest extent when it comes to speeding up asylum procedures when it comes to organizing returns faster, more efficient and sustainable. So that it doesn't stay that technical: It is unacceptable for the European Union to begin to renegotiate states like Morocco when it comes to taking back citizens who are not rightfully present here. It needs clarity, it needs closeness, but of course it also needs a perspective. That is to say, it takes the perspective for Morocco to be able to maintain good trade relations with the European Union, to have clear visa agreements and to build clear perspectives for local people. However, it is also necessary to clarify that, if there is no willingness to cooperate with the security authorities, the Commission, the Union, will not take note of this and will also assert its possibilities when it comes to showing strength, consistency and clarity. While we definitely express ourselves, the issue of distribution is often very bulky and emotionally debated. We are also against a distribution through the back door. [[Abg. Kassinger: That doesn't matter to us anyway!]] In contrast to the Commission, we are in favour of mandatory, flexible solidarity, which means that the 27 EU Member States are in a position to help and, in fact, contribute in accordance with their rules and possibilities. [[Abg. Belakovich: Depending on their possibilities! There's room!]] All other ways of reaching an agreement in the European Union are quite impossible. There are very clear rejections. But what all of us agree – whether we are visegrád states or not, from France to Bulgaria – is that it is important to make clear that if we are pursuing a closed common asylum and migration policy and are beginning to find ourselves where there is agreement, we have a chance to move forward. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Y ılmaz.]] – Yes, ladies and gentlemen! There is a clear addition to the question of faster asylum procedures. There is a high degree of agreement and willingness to strengthen the European Union's border protection. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Y ılmaz.]] I have recently spoken to the French Minister of the Interior, who is willing to invest more. In addition, there is a willingness – and these are already three points in which 27 EU Member States agree – to ensure an efficiently organised, clearly structured return, linked to return agreements – that is always important – so that there is also a perspective for the countries to cooperate with the European Union or the Member States of the European Union. This also applies, of course, to ethnic cleansings in the area of Rakhine against the Rohingya, which, when I was there, already lived considerably poorer, found much worse conditions for their lives. There was, for example, no telecommunications, no Internet, there was an incredible military presence, there was quite obviously oppression, and this joyous waiting for democracy and hopes for a life in freedom was already greatly dampened at that time. However, we are not limited to bilateral or European reactions, but we are also making it multilateral in international organisations, for example in special sessions of the Human Rights Council on European initiative, which we strongly supported. We are also very close to the American friends and the Asean partners. There needs to be an international printing scene, the Junta needs to notice that there is no escape here and that she is internationally at the prange. This protest, this printing scene, must continue. It was also said quite rightly: Democracy is, as it were, a retreat worldwide. Myanmar was one of the few cases where they said: Industriousness and resistance from our side are causing something. Now we have to be even more stubborn, we must not fall back. It must show: Fall-backs can happen, but they can never and never succeed. We've seen this difficult transition process in Myanmar, and we all know it wasn't perfect – the key to dealing with minorities in this country, because they still had a wide range of movement and development in front of them – but this transition process deserves us to support it and that it continues. – Thank you very much.
